Ruling 102 (101(a), ruling 94(f) applied): ktest-r4 and ktest-r8 mount one read-only export/graphs/<P> folder per late-50 tuning parent (38), ktest-r9 one per held-out parent of the plan (70) and the task list; none mounts export/ or export/graphs whole. 6 list(s) and record(s) read: every graph row in its step's plan set.

- ktest-r4: the tuning hand-off (step 5): answer_level.py's co_scores opens export/graphs/<P>/{pilot_graph,pilot_co,entities}.tsv for late-50 tasks absent from the CO tables (ruling 101(a))
- ktest-r8: the pre-check of the tuning hand-off (step 5): precheck_handoff.py reads the same graph files of the late-50 tuning parents (ruling 101(a))
- ktest-r9: the pre-check of the held-out hand-off (step 8): precheck_handoff.py reads the graph files of the 70 held-out parents and the task list; after the held-out run, a check of the hand-off, not training or ranking (ruling 101(a))
- ktest-r4 (mounts-B.json list): 38 of 38 parent folders
- ktest-r8 (mounts-B.json list): 38 of 38 parent folders
- ktest-r9 (mounts-B.json list): 70 of 70 parent folders
- ktest-r4 (ktest-r4.json): 38 of 38 parent folders
- ktest-r8 (ktest-r8.json): 38 of 38 parent folders
- ktest-r9 (ktest-r9.json): 70 of 70 parent folders

Ruling 35 (disclosure): while building the evaluation core, one builder's look at a temp file showed the names of files in two held-out commits (dated 2026-08-03). No truth was computed or used from it; tuning truth came from git for the June commits only.

Ruling 81 (disclosure): one container serves all 70 held-out parents, and it mounts export/ whole, so the graph and the facts of a later held-out parent, which hold the changes of earlier held-out task commits, are readable in it: 54 of the 70 exported held-out histories (export/graphs/<P>/ancestors.txt) name another held-out parent. The protection against truth from a later parent is per task, not per mount: a task is served only by the model trained on its own parent's graph (s = 1); candidates, comparators and packs are keyed by the task's own parent; and the audit shows for every task that the checkpoint used is its parent (ruling 60), that nothing was folded in (ruling 65) and, through the grader, that the task commit is not in that checkpoint's history (ruling 73). One container per parent is not built.

Ruling 89 (a limit of the isolation, not a clean pass): the stage-B container kge-B1 (its docker inspect record orchestrator/kge-B1-inspect-20261114T190928Z.json, sha256 052cbfa0ec863e3bb9e2069e99eb97b58a358cb53ded44f803d73dde8d4235e6) mounted eval/ and export/ read-only and train/; export/ also holds the exported graphs of the 70 held-out parents, which contain the changes of held-out task commits, so held-out material was mounted in a training container from the first stage-B unit on. That cannot be undone; the rule that holds is per use. Parts: 2385 stage-B parts (planned 2385), each meta.json naming its checkpoint: all among the plan's 53 tuning checkpoints; 210 held-out parts checked against the plan's 70 held-out checkpoints. Code: the frozen stage-B code loads a graph only by a checkpoint of the plan or a task's own parent (graph loads at train/stageB.py:42, train/stageB.py:147, train/stageB.py:150, train/stageB.py:208, train/stageB.py:298, train/stageB.py:312, train/stageB.py:394, train/kge.py:72, train/kge.py:109, train/kge.py:245, train/kge.py:610, train/kge.py:658; every site in leak_audit.json ruling89.sites), and the constants it trains on (STAGEA_SHA cd806c803c4a, LARGEST_TUNE 7b3a74528189) are tuning checkpoints. Ruling 90: the one read of held-out parents' folders, train/stageB.py:160 (make_plan, once, when plan.json was written: ancestors.txt and sizes.json of each held-out parent, export/checkpoints.py:117 and :122, for the plan's held-out assignment and lag; no triple, path or co-change pair), is by design and listed here, not a failure. The same rule covers the later scoring container that mounts export/ (train/heldout.py: 25 graph sites, each keyed by a checkpoint of the plan, a task's own parent or the probe's synthetic graph).

Sibling seeds (disclosure): the held-out rows a run reads are truth-free one by one, but the task rows of one parent together list most of that commit's changed files (item 4). Ranking a task reads only its own row: no code path uses a sibling seed (by_ck groups tasks per checkpoint only to batch them; scoring takes one task's id, seed and candidates).
